# Review log: Lenny-issued bearer tokens verify only on the process that signed them

## Standing context

### Settled
- OD3 is settled as option (c) by the owner's delegate (2026-10-24, D26): §17.4 item 3 stays universal, `lenny-ops` stays on `--production` in CODE-6, and the mismatch is a Defects entry for a direct code fix after this proposal lands. Do not re-raise it.

- Owner decision 1 (2026-10-23): production signing is remote asymmetric KMS signing, and every verifier verifies locally against public keys selected by `kid`. Rejected: one shared HS256 key sealed by the KMS, and a per-replica KMS-signed intermediate key as a default.
- Owner decision 2 (2026-10-23): Lenny-issued tokens have a fixed lifetime, the gateway never re-mints per interaction, and holders refresh by pulling.
- Owner decision 3 (2026-10-23): the default capability token lifetime is 15 minutes, operator-tunable within the bounds SPEC-3 Edit 1 states (`120 ≤ ttl ≤ 3600`).
- Owner decision 4 (2026-10-23): option B. Home: SPEC-11 Edit 2 (**Revocation set**); implementation: CODE-6 item 6; reasons, residual risks, and rejected options: summary D22.
- Owner decision 5 (2026-10-23): the 15-minute default governs every Lenny-issued token whose holder can refresh it by pulling: playground bearers and `POST /v1/oauth/token` tokens requested without `expires_in` (user bearers from rotation and scope narrowing, and operability-scope tokens). An explicit `expires_in` is honoured within the per-dialect caps. The session-creation `session_capability` token and the delegation `actor_token` path keep their caps. The gateway-to-pod renewal path is a follow-up proposal. Home: SPEC-3 Edit 1; implementation: CODE-8 items 3 to 5; summary D13.
- OD1 (owner's delegate, 2026-10-24): option (a). `lenny-ops` reuses the gateway's `authmw` unchanged and emits the gateway's existing revoked-token response, `401 TOKEN_REVOKED`. SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 3 refers to the response a gateway replica returns for a revoked token and names no code, because §15 defines no revoked-token code and §13.3's lowercase `token_revoked` is an informal mention. The §15 row and the §13.3 spelling are a follow-up recorded under the summary's Defects. Home: SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 3; tests: the CODE-6 rule-3 case and TEST-1 items 2 and 6; summary D23.
- Owner decision 6 (owner, 2026-10-24), answering OD2 with option (b): `lenny-ops` production readiness passes when either Postgres or the Kubernetes API is reachable. Home: SPEC-11 Edit 6 (§25.4 "Probes"); implementation: CODE-13 (the permissive `/healthz` handler, the harness wait, and the production readiness probe); tests: CODE-13's tier-1 and Helm cases and the readiness assertions in TEST-1 item 2; summary D24. Accepted cost: a replica whose own Postgres connection broke while its peers are healthy stays in rotation with `degradation.warnings`. Rejected: deferring the probe fix to a follow-up.
- Step (1a) passes a token with no `issued_tokens` row and rejects only on `revoked_at`. The round-1 fail-closed missing-row rule is withdrawn ([spec.2.fix-G1.1] and [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1] CORRECTS entries), because playground and impersonation bearers have no row and are the delegation `subject_token`, so the rule rejected every delegation from those sessions.
- The client-side per-replica KMS sign rate limit is deleted (round 4, [spec.4.fix-G2.1], summary D15). A static split of a fleet quota across replicas refuses demand the quota could serve, because the Token Service does not autoscale, and the provider throttle already returns the same retryable 503 fleet-wide without tripping the breaker. This departs from the problem statement's scope line "There is also a per-replica sign rate limit" as an explicit decision.
- Scope departures recorded as decisions in the summary: the per-replica limit (D15), the §12.2 rows (D20), key-identity flags and values replaced by the reserved aliases `platform:jwt-signing` and `platform:jwt-verify` (D5), and the verifier refresh rule replaced by a startup-loaded static key set (D8).
- The §25.15 closing paragraph ("the core operational loop ... continues to function") stays true under owner decision 4 for a `lenny-ops` that loaded its revocation set before the outage, so it needs no edit. This closes the round-4 OPEN on that paragraph.
- SPEC-11 Edit 5 (the `/v1/admin/me` Degradation qualification) is retired, because under owner decision 4 the unqualified sentence holds for every caller `lenny-ops` authenticates; the cold-start refusal lives in SPEC-11 Edit 2.
- The §25.4 GatewayClient holds the projected ServiceAccount token (`pkg/ops/gateway/token.go` `FileTokenLoader`; `ops-deployment.yaml` `expirationSeconds: 3600`), and no `lenny-ops` code requests a `POST /v1/oauth/token` token. `security.oidc.minTokenTTLSeconds` therefore never meets a token that `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` governs, and the 120 s floor has no conflict. SPEC-8's GatewayClient refresh-lead example is withdrawn. The spec-versus-chart default (300 s versus 600 s) predates 0097.
- SPEC-8 Edit 2 states the KMS Sign rate formula with no worked Tier figures and no credential-lease term. Home: SPEC-8 Edit 2; reasons: summary D16; ledger [redesign.2]. This closes the Open on the 230/s and 690/s figures.
- `lenny-ops` refuses the playground bearer and accepts every other Lenny-issued class, an impersonation bearer included. Home: SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 7; implementation: CODE-6 item 6; reasons and rejected options: summary D25; ledger [redesign.2].

### Open

- (separate finding, predates 0097) `issued_tokens` has no StoreRouter routing rule, and §25.4 "Storage Routing" has no row for the `lenny-ops` `issued_tokens` read.
- (separate finding, predates 0097) Production `lenny-ops` verifies no upstream IdP token in code today, so SPEC-11 Edit 2's IdP clause is unimplemented.
- (separate finding, predates 0097) A revoked playground or impersonation bearer presented as a `subject_token` is not checked against `pg:revoked` or the impersonation ticket store by the Token Service, and impersonation bearers have no `issued_tokens` row for §11.4 item 5.
- (separate finding, predates 0097) §4.9 "Upstream egress" says all other gateway egress is blocked, which was already false.
- (checklist) Whether S20 (CODE-7) still reaches tiers 3 and 7a was not checked; the CODE-7 tests listed are tier 2.

### Traps

- Before accepting any fail-closed rule on a path, trace every caller of that path. Round 1 (the step (1a) missing-row rule broke delegation from playground and impersonation sessions) and round 4 (the `lenny-ops` refusal during a Postgres outage broke the §25.15 loop) both broke a downstream flow by not doing so.
- Do not re-file the per-replica sign rate limit, the step (1a) missing-row rule, or the D13 scope. Under owner decision 5 the D13 scope is playground bearers plus `POST /v1/oauth/token` tokens requested without `expires_in`; do not narrow it back to playground bearers, and do not extend it to the session-creation token or delegated children.
- Do not wire the gateway's `RevocationFreshness` staleness gate into `lenny-ops`; it implements the rejected option A.
- Do not cite "the mechanisms the gateway's revocation cache uses" as a whole. §13.3 also holds the per-request fallback and the fail-closed refusal, which `lenny-ops` does not apply (SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 5), and it states no periodic read.
- Do not restore a 1-hour lifetime bound on the `lenny-ops` missed-revocation window, and do not wire `lenny-ops` without the chart's Postgres DSN; see summary D22 and CODE-6 item 6.
- Do not re-file a credential-lease issuance or lease-renewal term in the SPEC-8 sign-rate formula, and do not file a KMS dependency of lease renewal. A credential-lease token is an opaque `TokenStore` handle; see ledger [redesign.2]. Do not restore worked Tier figures without a spec source for the holder count.
- Do not re-file refusing impersonation bearers on `lenny-ops` or a Redis-backed playground check on `lenny-ops`; see summary D25.
- Do not restore a literal response code in SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 4 or rule 7; see summary D23.

### Deferred

Items the next non-spec loop absorbs:

- DOCS-2 "State and resets" mirrors: docs/getting-started/quickstart.md "Step 1: Start the embedded stack", docs/operator-guide/installation.md "`lenny up` for local evaluation", and docs/runtime-author-guide/local-development.md (mode table and the "`lenny up`" paragraph) drop KMS or key management from the in-memory store list and keep the "no separate ... KMS ... process" clause ([spec.1.fix-G3.1], [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]).
- docs/operator-guide/security.md and docs/getting-started/architecture.md still name the admin credential as the only in-process mint ([spec.2.fix-design-G1.1] OPEN).
- docs/runbooks/gateway-replica-failure.md needs the KMS key-set startup-exit cause, and docs/ should be swept for "dev HMAC" ([spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1]).
- The cloud-provider upgrade edge case: the chart refuses to render until `kms.signQuotaPerSecond` is set and `kms.endpointCIDR` names the private KMS endpoint subnet, which also blocks the Token Service upgrade; CHART-4's `values.yaml` comment names the private endpoint subnet; D19 gains the private-endpoint reason ([spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]).

Absorbed on 2026-10-23 (owner-decisions fixer): CODE-9 now targets `cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go` and its test fake (CODE-9 item 4); CHART-1 moves the schema minimum and `schema_test.go` to 120; checklist S1, S3, S11, S14, S18, S20, S21, and S27 are reworded, S26 is removed, and S7 depends on S1.

## Ledger

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` governs only the bearers `POST /v1/playground/token` mints; the session-creation `session_capability` token (pods present it; it is the delegation `actor_token`) keeps its per-dialect cap — BECAUSE pods cannot reach /v1/oauth/token and a session_capability rotation copies typ and caps exp at subject.exp (pkg/tokenexchange/tokenexchange.go header comment and Derive), so a 900 s session token would break pod→gateway calls and delegate_task 15 minutes into a session (sessions run up to maxSessionAgeSeconds 7200, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:414) — ALTERNATIVES: gateway-side renewal timer (new mechanism for an out-of-scope, unimplemented mint); move pod auth to the SA token (leaves actor_token bounding delegation trees); user_bearer→session_capability grant (breaks typ immutability, spec/10:227); bound by maxSessionAgeSeconds (active-time cap that pauses in suspension).
WATCHOUT: Owner decision 2 in the problem statement names "/v1/oauth/token or the playground endpoint" as holder refresh paths. SPEC-3 Edit 1 no longer names /v1/oauth/token because no TTL-governed token renews through it; this narrows the scope of the 15-minute lifetime, it does not reopen decisions 2 or 3 (fixed lifetime, no re-mint, 900 default all hold). Do not re-add the /v1/oauth/token renewal clause or apply the TTL to the session-creation token without a renewal mechanism. — EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md "Owner decisions" item 2; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:223
WATCHOUT: The knob name `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` now governs exactly the tokens `playground.bearerTtlSeconds` governed. A later finding may propose reverting the rename; it was kept because it matches owner decision 3's term and reverting cascades into CODE-8, CHART-1, DOCS-2, SPEC-3 Edits 3–6, SPEC-8, and the checklist. — EVIDENCE: summary.md D13
DECISION: Floor raised to 120 (`120 ≤ ttl ≤ 3600`) in the one home, SPEC-3 Edit 1; §27.2 row, §27.3.1 sentence, §17.8.1 row, CODE-8, CHART-1, and DOCS-2 drop their copies or point at SPEC-3 Edit 1 — BECAUSE ttl − 60 in the SPEC-8 sign-rate formula is zero at ttl = 60 and §27.3.1 fixes the client lead at 60 s (spec/27_web-playground.md:141) — ALTERNATIVES: writing 120 into every copy (keeps several homes).
FACT: §17.6 item 6 states no lifetime for the bootstrap admin credential; the 10-year value lives only in code, `DefaultTokenTTL = 10 * 365 * 24 * time.Hour` in pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go. SPEC-3 Edit 1 now states it in §10.2, which makes SPEC-2 step 2's "Its lifetime exceeds the step-3 wait" rest on spec text. — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:494-512
FACT: Playground mint invariant 4 (spec/10:259) now points at the "JWT claims" `expiry` rule (SPEC-3 Edit 2); CODE-8 item 2 cites SPEC-3 Edit 1 as the exp home. The dev-mode `exp = now + ttl` in the §27.3.1 Auth-by-mode row (SPEC-3 Edit 6) is left as is: dev has no subject and the per-dialect cap never binds below 3600.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: The session-creation `session_capability` mint stays a gateway in-process mint and is bound to §13.3 write-before-issue statements (1)-(3) by one SPEC-1 "Signing topology" sentence, with `session_mint` added to the §16.7 `token.exchanged` exchange_type enum (SPEC-1 Edit 5). BECAUSE: SPEC-6 removes "Session tokens" from §4.3 item 3, which drops the issued_tokens row and audit that §4.3 plus §13.3 ("Internal token issuance paths ... flow through the Token Service", spec/13_security-model.md:656) implied, and §11.4 item 5 revokes through issued_tokens (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:274). The admin rotation is the precedent: a gateway in-process mint that records both rows (spec/13_security-model.md:664). ALTERNATIVES: moving session tokens back to the Token Service needs a tokensv1 mint RPC (summary non-goal); a row without the audit row breaks the §13.3 "no token without an audit row" invariant.
WATCHOUT: The recording sentence names statements (1), (2), and (3) and never step (1a). A session mint has no `subject_token` to re-read, so writing "under the write-before-issue ordering" unqualified would lead an implementor to run (1a) on the authenticating token. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1 Edit 1 "Signing topology".
WATCHOUT: Do not extend the recording rule to impersonation bearers. A `token.exchanged` row for a cross-tenant mint falsifies "The `token.exchanged` audit event therefore always carries a single `tenant_id`" (spec/13_security-model.md:650). Under the fail-closed step (1a), impersonation and playground bearers cannot be exchanged, which matches "NOT routed through /v1/oauth/token". EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:650.
DECISION: Step (1a) fails closed on a missing row for a token the verifier key set verified, reusing `subject_token_revoked`/`actor_token_revoked`. BECAUSE: as staged it passed every row-less token, including a revoked playground bearer (Redis `pg:revoked`, spec/27_web-playground.md:89,96). ALTERNATIVES: a new reason `subject_token_unrecorded` adds protocol surface for a case treated as revoked.
WATCHOUT: `session_mint` has one home, the §16.7 enum in SPEC-1 Edit 5. SPEC-1 Edit 1 names the audit row but not the value. Keep it that way.
FACT: The session-creation mint and its recording have no CODE deliverable and no test in this proposal (summary "Defects in the shipped tree": pkg/gateway/sessionserver has no signer). The later session-mint fix owns a tier-4 test asserting the issued_tokens row and the `session_mint` audit row commit before the token returns.
DECISION: §4.3 item 3 is the home for what the Token Service mints and §10.2 "Signing topology" for what the gateway mints in process; SPEC-1's Token Service list is cut to a pointer and SPEC-6's inserted sentence points at the §10.2 list. D9 reworded. BECAUSE: §4.3 item 3 is the tree's existing minted-here list and SPEC-6 already edits it.
DECISION: The `typ` stamping sentence (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:227) is restaged as SPEC-1 Edit 4: "The minting component stamps `typ` alongside the other claims when it signs the token". BECAUSE: SPEC-7 moves signing ahead of the transaction and SPEC-1 makes the Token Service a signer. Left "minted on every gateway-issued JWT" (line 221) alone: the Token Service already minted a2a_delegation and service_token under the current §4.3.
DECISION: SPEC-7 Edit 2's restatement of the subject_token_revoked rule and the closing "step (1a) re-read rejects ..." clause are deleted; step (1a) in Edit 1 is the one home. The exp-clamp half of the closing sentence stays, because it replaces the current "re-read happens inside the same advisory-locked transaction as the signing step" justification.

### [spec.1.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: One KMS egress peer (`kms.endpointCIDR`, TCP 443) for the gateway and `lenny-ops`, rendered whenever `kms.provider` is not `local`, with a chart render refusal of `0.0.0.0/0` or `::/0` on a cloud provider. Normative home is SPEC-10 Edit 1 (§13.2.4 Gateway row); lenny-ops row, §25.4 YAML comment, §17.1 row, CHART-4, and D19 point at it — BECAUSE the gateway's only external 443 path (`allow-gateway-egress-llm-upstream`) renders only with a proxy-mode pool (spec/13_security-model.md:389), excepts RFC1918, and can be narrowed by `egressCIDRs.llmProviders`, so a gateway could not reach KMS for `LoadKeySet`/`Sign` — ALTERNATIVES: private-endpoint-only condition for the gateway (crashloops on public endpoint without proxy pool); copy allow-token-service's always-rendered 0.0.0.0/0 peer (cancels NET-057 excepts, spec/13:475); render llm-upstream unconditionally (broadens egress, still blocks private endpoints); preflight check instead of render fail (CHART-1 already uses render-time fail for signQuotaPerSecond).
WATCHOUT: The retired option "lenny-ops KMS peer only when endpointCIDR is narrower than 0.0.0.0/0; a public KMS endpoint passes the external HTTPS/webhook rule" lost because that reasoning holds for lenny-ops but never for the gateway, and under the render refusal the narrower-than condition is always true (a dead second condition). Do not reintroduce it. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:275 (Gateway row has no KMS peer), :389 (llm-upstream render condition)
FACT: The §13.2.4 Gateway row anchor "`kube-system` CoreDNS (UDP/TCP 53); external HTTPS (TCP 443, rendered" occurs once in spec/; the only chart KMS peer today is in `allow-token-service` (charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml:271); values.yaml:894-897 comment claims the 0.0.0.0/0 default is "bounded by the per-port (443) egress rule". — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:894
WATCHOUT: Existing helm unittests set a cloud provider without `kms.endpointCIDR` (charts/lenny/tests/token-service-deployment_test.yaml:166 `kms.provider: aws`; gateway-deployment_test.yaml:736 `provider: azure`). helm-unittest renders only the templates a suite names, so the guard in system-network-policies.yaml/ops-network-policies.yaml will not fire there, but any suite that renders those templates under a cloud provider must set a narrow CIDR. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/tests/token-service-deployment_test.yaml:166
FACT: The Token Service row and `allow-token-service` peer are unchanged; under the refusal its rendered CIDR is simply the narrow operator value. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:276

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds governs only the bearers POST /v1/playground/token mints. The session-creation session_capability token (the token pods present, and the parent token used as the §8.2 delegation actor_token) keeps its per-dialect cap, the same way D13 already exempts delegated children — BECAUSE no holder of that token has a refresh path: pods cannot reach /v1/oauth/token, and a rotation of a session_capability token is capped at subject.exp and copies typ (pkg/tokenexchange/tokenexchange.go:232-260). A 900 s cap would break pod→gateway calls and delegate_task (actor_token_expired) 15 minutes into a 7200 s session — ALTERNATIVES: (a) a gateway-side timer that renews each session's token (new per-session state, has to survive coordinator handoff; rejected as a large new mechanism for a mint path that is out of scope and unimplemented); (b) moving pod auth to the SA token only (it does not fix the delegation actor, whose exp still bounds the whole tree); (c) building the actor from session state in place of a token (a large §8.2/§13.3 rewrite that weakens the actor_token_expired predicate); (d) letting a user_bearer exchange mint a session_capability (contradicts typ immutability at spec/10:227 and spec/13:646).
DECISION: the lower bound rises to 120, so ttl − 60 (the §27.3.1 client refresh lead) is at least 60 s. SPEC-3 Edit 1 is the only site that states the bound. The §27.2 row, §27.3.1 Edit 4, and the §17.8.1 row drop their copies.
DECISION: SPEC-3 Edit 1 now states the bootstrap admin credential's 10-year lifetime, matching pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go DefaultTokenTTL. §17.6 states no lifetime, so the old citation pointed at nothing.
FACT: playground bearers are typ session_capability with no session_id (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go completeMint). The shipped code sets exp = now + BearerTTL and does not apply the subject-exp term. CODE-8 item 2 now points at the SPEC-3 Edit 1 rule, which carries all three terms — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go:259
FACT: maxSessionAgeSeconds caps active time only and pauses while the session is suspended (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:414). It is therefore the wrong wall-clock bound for a session token, so the session-creation token gets the per-dialect cap — EVIDENCE: spec/05:414
WATCHOUT: under this design the knob governs the same tokens playground.bearerTtlSeconds governed, so the rename is cosmetic and D13 justifies it only by owner decision 3's name. A later round may file "revert the rename". That revert would cascade into CODE-8, CHART-1, DOCS-2, SPEC-3 Edits 3-6, SPEC-8, and the checklist. Do not do it piecemeal.
WATCHOUT: the SPEC-8 Tier-3 example still uses 10,000 holders. Under this design that figure counts every Tier-3 session as a playground holder, which makes it an upper bound. It is not wrong, but a capacity lens may question it.
OPEN: whether session-creation session_capability tokens should eventually carry the 15-minute lifetime (which needs a gateway-side renewal of the session's token for pods and the delegation actor) belongs to the follow-up proposal that implements the session-creation mint and delegation signing. It is not an owner question for 0097.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: session-creation `session_capability` stays a gateway in-process mint, and SPEC-1 "Signing topology" gains one clause: the gateway records it in `issued_tokens` with its `token.exchanged` row under statements (1)-(3) of §13.3 "Write-before-issue ordering". §16.7 gains `exchange_type: session_mint` — BECAUSE the bootstrap admin rotation is already a gateway in-process mint that records both rows (spec/13_security-model.md:664, cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go adminRotationExchangeType), so this extends an existing pattern — ALTERNATIVES: keep "Session tokens" in §4.3 at the Token Service (rejected: playground bearers are already gateway-minted session-capability JWTs, the restaged "After authentication, the gateway mints a signed JWT" would contradict §4.3 again, and it needs the Token Service mint RPC the non-goal at summary.md:64 rejects). Extending the rule to impersonation bearers was also rejected: their missing row predates this proposal, and recording `token.exchanged` for a cross-tenant mint breaks spec/13_security-model.md:650 "therefore always carries a single tenant_id".
DECISION: step (1a) fails closed. A token that the verifier key set verified and that has no `issued_tokens` row is rejected with the same `*_token_revoked` reason. Only a token the key set did not verify (an upstream IdP token) passes on a missing row — BECAUSE playground bearers (revoked in Redis `pg:revoked`, spec/27_web-playground.md:96) and impersonation bearers have no row, so a revoked playground bearer presented to /v1/oauth/token would otherwise mint a fresh recorded token — ALTERNATIVES: a new reason code (rejected: new surface for a case that is treated as revoked).
FACT: issued_tokens rows outlive a token's exp by the trailing hour (§13.3 "Authoritative durability"; GC, erasure, and tenant deletion Phase 4 delete only past it), so the missing-row rejection never fires on a valid, recorded token — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:676
FACT: no gateway path mints a `user_bearer` today; the only gateway mints are impersonation (no row, pkg/gateway/credentials/impersonation/service.go), admin (row, pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go IssuedTokens), and playground (no row, pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go). The §10.2 "bootstrap OIDC login path" user_bearer mint has no code.
WATCHOUT: once step (1a) fails closed, a later "bootstrap OIDC login" user_bearer mint that writes no row would make that bearer impossible to rotate. Whoever implements that path must record the row — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:222
WATCHOUT: RecordWithRotationAudit's ErrNoRows branch (pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go "Previous token absent or already revoked") is reachable after CODE-9 only for an IdP-issued subject, so CODE-9 item 3 must say that rather than "a missing row".
DECISION: finding 2 makes §4.3 item 3 the home for Token Service mints and §10.2 "Signing topology" the home for gateway mints. Each cites the other, and D9 is reworded to match — BECAUSE §4.3 is the tree's existing list ("minted here"), so the proposal's duplicate in §10.2 is the copy to remove.
OPEN: impersonation bearers have no issued_tokens row, so §11.4 item 5 user invalidation cannot revoke them durably. This predates 0097 and is separate from G2.
DEFERRED [0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.implementation-checklist.md]: step S1 says SPEC-1 "Lands the §10.2 signing model, signing topology, and playground signer edits". After G2, SPEC-1 also carries Edit 4 (the §10.2 `typ` stamping sentence) and Edit 5 (§16.7 `token.exchanged` `session_mint`), so S1 should name SPEC-1 without listing its parts, or add those two.

DECISION: devTenantId rows read "dev-mode playground JWT", not "dev JWT" — BECAUSE "dev JWT" collides with the CLI dev bearer that the gateway trusts through `--bearer-trust-hmac-key-file`, which stays HMAC.
WATCHOUT: HMAC wording about the dev bearer-trust key (charts/lenny/presets/dev.yaml, gateway-deployment.yaml comments, the §17.4 banner's "trusted dev key") is correct after this proposal; only the playground-JWT HMAC wording is wrong. EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:194, summary D12.
FACT: chart default `kms.provider` is "local" but nothing ties `global.devMode`/`LENNY_DEV_MODE` to it, so staged text must not say dev mode signs "with the local provider key". EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:879, spec/27_web-playground.md:61.
FACT: DOCS-2 already removes "dev HMAC JWT" from docs/reference/configuration.md's `playground.devTenantId` row. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:179.

The docs mirrors (quickstart.md:58, installation.md:33, local-development.md:18 and :32) drop key management/KMS from their in-memory store list in DOCS-2. Their "no separate ... KMS ... process" clause stays, because it remains true.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: one KMS egress rule for the gateway and lenny-ops. The peer is `kms.endpointCIDR` on TCP 443, rendered when `kms.provider` is not `local`, and the chart refuses to render on a cloud provider while `kms.endpointCIDR` is `0.0.0.0/0` or `::/0`. The rule's home is the §13.2.4 Gateway row (SPEC-10 Edit 1). The lenny-ops row and the §25.4 YAML comment point to it — BECAUSE the gateway has no unconditional external-HTTPS rule (llm-upstream renders only with proxy pools, and `egressCIDRs.llmProviders` can narrow it), so "a public endpoint passes the external 443 rule" (old D19) holds for lenny-ops only. Requiring a narrow CIDR also makes lenny-ops's "narrower than 0.0.0.0/0" condition always true, so keeping two conditions would be hair — ALTERNATIVES: (a) a gateway peer only when the CIDR is narrower, as lenny-ops had it: a default cloud install with a public endpoint and no proxy pool then crashloops at LoadKeySet; (b) copy the Token Service's always-rendered peer with default 0.0.0.0/0: NetworkPolicy allows are additive, so the peer cancels the llm-upstream excepts, which violates NET-057; (c) render llm-upstream unconditionally: this broadens gateway egress for no-proxy installs, and llmProviders narrowing would still block KMS; (d) a 0.0.0.0/0-with-excepts KMS peer: this re-broadens an llmProviders-narrowed gateway, which defeats the compliance narrowing.
FACT: the gateway already takes `--kms-provider` and wraps §4.9 connector credentials through KMS (values.yaml kms comment, gateway-deployment.yaml --kms-provider). Its missing KMS egress therefore predates 0097, and 0097 makes it fatal at startup. allow-gateway-egress has no KMS peer, and allow-token-service always renders `kms.endpointCIDR` — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml (allow-token-service ipBlock kms.endpointCIDR; allow-gateway-egress ends at kube-dns); charts/lenny/templates/gateway-llm-upstream-egress.yaml is the separate llm-upstream template.
FACT: the §17.1 NetworkPolicies row lists only lenny-ops policies, and no gateway-egress row exists in §17.1. The gateway clause therefore does not go into §17.1 — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md §17.1 table, NetworkPolicies row.
WATCHOUT: helm-unittest renders only the templates a suite lists. Put the endpointCIDR `fail` in a template that every cloud-provider render reaches (or in a helper that system-network-policies.yaml and ops-network-policies.yaml both call). Existing suites that set `kms.provider: aws` and render those templates need `kms.endpointCIDR` set — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/tests/gateway-deployment_test.yaml, token-service-deployment_test.yaml set a cloud provider.
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S27 reads "Lands the `lenny-ops` KMS egress rule", but CHART-4 now lands the gateway and lenny-ops KMS egress peers and the cloud-provider `kms.endpointCIDR` render guard. Reword S27 to "Lands the gateway and `lenny-ops` KMS egress rule" (dependencies S10, S25 unchanged).
OPEN: spec/04 §4.9 "Upstream egress" says all gateway egress other than llm-upstream "including cluster-internal CIDRs ... is blocked". That was already false (PgBouncer, Redis, apiserver, Token Service) before this fix. It is a separate finding for a later round.

### [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
Corrected (spec-changes.md only):
- SPEC-8 Edit 2 "KMS Sign rate": Tier 3 example input "10,000 holders" -> "at most 10,000 playground bearer holders, the Tier 3 session ceiling" (an upper bound from a §16.5 tier parameter, so "terms come from §16.5" holds; figures 230/s and 690/s unchanged).
- SPEC-10 Edit 1 (§13.2.4 Gateway row): after the 0.0.0.0/0 refusal, added "so a cloud deployment sets it to the subnet of the provider's private KMS endpoint".
DECISION: keep the chart-wide 0.0.0.0/0 refusal and require the private KMS endpoint subnet on cloud providers — BECAUSE the gateway's only external-HTTPS rule (allow-gateway-egress-llm-upstream) renders only under gateway.llmUpstreamEgress.enabled, so the gateway needs its own KMS peer, and a public regional KMS endpoint spans provider ranges no single CIDR covers — ALTERNATIVES: list-valued kms.endpointCIDR (provider ranges for KMS are not published narrowly: AWS ip-ranges has no KMS service key, GCP uses all Google API ranges, Azure's AzureKeyVault tag is large; also forces a type change on the always-rendered allow-token-service peer); scoping the refusal to gateway/lenny-ops peers (the value is shared, so the gateway peer would still be unrestricted); omitting the peer when unrestricted (fails when llmUpstreamEgress is disabled or egressCIDRs.llmProviders is narrowed).
FACT: gateway external HTTPS egress is conditional — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/gateway-llm-upstream-egress.yaml:22 `{{- if .Values.gateway.llmUpstreamEgress.enabled }}`; allow-gateway-egress (system-network-policies.yaml:535) has no 443 internet rule.
WATCHOUT: the refusal breaks `helm upgrade` for every cloud-provider install on the shipped default endpointCIDR "0.0.0.0/0" (charts/lenny/values.yaml:897), Token Service included, exactly as CHART-1 item 2's required kms.signQuotaPerSecond already does — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-10 Edit 1; non-spec-changes.md CHART-1 item 2.
DEFERRED [summary.md]: D19 gives the reason for the refusal but not for the private-endpoint requirement SPEC-10 Edit 1 now states; append "and a public KMS endpoint spans provider ranges no single CIDR covers, so a cloud deployment uses the private endpoint's subnet".
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, Edge cases]: add "**Upgrade of a cloud-provider install.** The chart refuses to render until `kms.signQuotaPerSecond` is set and `kms.endpointCIDR` names the private KMS endpoint subnet (SPEC-10 Edit 1); the shipped `0.0.0.0/0` default fails, which also blocks the Token Service upgrade." CHART-4's values.yaml comment should name the private endpoint subnet rather than only "a narrower CIDR".
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, DOCS-2 + Files touched]: SPEC-9 Edit 3 removes KMS from the §17.4 in-memory store list, but docs/getting-started/quickstart.md:58 ("Postgres, Redis, and KMS"), docs/operator-guide/installation.md:33 and docs/runtime-author-guide/local-development.md:32 ("Postgres, Redis, key management, and artifact storage") still list it; add a DOCS-2 bullet removing KMS/key management from those lists (the local KMS is keyed by the chart-generated master key Secret) and add the three paths to files-touched. Not landed: authoring is outside this loop's non-spec grant.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, CODE-9 tests]: the tier-2 issuedtokenstore cases cannot pin the Lenny-vs-IdP classification, which persistIssuedToken makes (CODE-9 item 1). Add tier-1 pkg/tokenservice case: key-set-verified subject's jti is in the recheck list, upstream-verified subject's is not, and a key-set-verified subject whose fake store reports a missing row returns 400 invalid_grant subject_token_revoked.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, CODE-9 Targets + Files touched]: RecordWithAudit is also declared in the adminIssuedTokenStore interface and called from cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go (:177, :269) with a fake in user_revocation_test.go (:247); add both to CODE-9 targets (the admin-rotation call passes an empty recheck list) and to the cmd/lenny-gateway files-touched list.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: Tier-3 replica counts that size the SPEC-8 per-replica sign limit are gateway 5 min / 30 max and Token Service 4 — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md §17.8.2 "Gateway replicas (min / max)" row and "Operational defaults by tier" Token Service replicas row
DECISION: filed SPEC-8/SPEC-4/SPEC-5 per-replica limit floor(Q/(G+T)) with G = HPA max as a design defect — BECAUSE at min scale the fleet sign capacity is (5+4)/34 of Q (about 180/s at Q=690), below SPEC-8's own 230/s sustained figure, and rate_limited is excluded from both alerts so the refusals are silent — ALTERNATIVES: a separate alert-only finding (rejected, same root)

FACT: SPEC-7 replaces the Redis-backed revocation-cache read with a Postgres issued_tokens re-read inside the lock; this is more durable under a Redis reset, and the sign moved outside the lock removes the per-tenant serialization — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md §13.3 "Actor-token freshness under concurrent rotation"

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: Step (1a) re-reads only the `issued_tokens` rows that exist and rejects only on `revoked_at`; a token with no row passes (SPEC-7 Edit 1). CODE-9 lists the subject and actor `jti` unconditionally and fails only on `revoked_at` — BECAUSE playground bearers (revoked in Redis `pg:revoked`, spec/27 §27.3.1 "Per-request revocation check") and impersonation bearers (ticket store, pkg/gateway/credentials/impersonation/service.go) never have rows, so failing closed on a missing row rejected every §8.2 delegation whose `subject_token` is such a bearer (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md "Child-token minting": subject_token is the authenticated user's JWT) — ALTERNATIVES: external-only rejection (Token Service has one handler for internal and external exchanges, no predicate); exempting by `origin`/typ (enumerates exceptions; each future unrecorded mint becomes another); recording playground/impersonation bearers (dual-write of playground revocation; cross-tenant impersonation breaks the single-`tenant_id` token.exchanged rule, spec/13 §13.3); actor-only missing-row rule (no consumer).
WATCHOUT: The withdrawn fail-closed missing-row rule (round 1, [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]) lost because "verified by the key set" does not imply "has an issued_tokens row": playground and impersonation bearers are key-set-verified with no row. Do not reintroduce it in a narrower form (external-only, by origin, typ, or caller), and do not add a step (1a) sentence saying where playground/impersonation revocation is checked for a subject_token: no spec section or code path does that today. — EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md:96-97; spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:59; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go (jti recorded only on the oidc session record)
OPEN: A revoked playground or impersonation bearer presented as a `subject_token` to /v1/oauth/token (or as a delegation subject) is not checked against `pg:revoked` or the impersonation ticket store by the Token Service. This predates 0097 and no staged edit addresses it; a later proposal owns it.
FACT: RecordWithRotationAudit's `pgx.ErrNoRows` branch ("Previous token absent or already revoked") is reachable after CODE-9 only for a subject with no row (any row-less subject, playground included, not only IdP). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go:276
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G2.1]: the DECISION "Step (1a) fails closed on a missing row" and the WATCHOUT clause "Under the fail-closed step (1a), impersonation and playground bearers cannot be exchanged" are withdrawn; a row-less token passes step (1a).
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]: the DECISION "step (1a) fails closed", the WATCHOUT that a row-less bootstrap-OIDC user_bearer would be impossible to rotate, and the WATCHOUT that the ErrNoRows branch is reachable "only for an IdP-issued subject" are wrong; row-less tokens pass and the ErrNoRows branch is reachable for any row-less subject.
CORRECTS [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]: the DEFERRED tier-1 pkg/tokenservice Lenny-vs-IdP classification case is moot; CODE-9 no longer classifies.
DECISION: §13.3 "Token issuance and rotation" exception sentence is staged as SPEC-7 Edit 5 and points at §10.2 "Signing topology" rather than listing the mints — BECAUSE "Signing topology" is the one home for the gateway's in-process mint set (D9) and SPEC-7 already owns §13.3, so checklist S7 and Spec-files-touched stay true — ALTERNATIVES: enumerating the mints in §13.3 (second home); staging in SPEC-6 (makes checklist S6 stale, adds spec/13 to SPEC-6); deleting the sentence (loses the admin-rotation parenthetical's referent).
USEFUL [spec.1.fix-G2.1]: its OPEN on spec/13_security-model.md:616 "the one exception" named the exact sentence.
WATCHOUT: docs/getting-started/architecture.md:192 and docs/operator-guide/security.md:108 and :114 still say the bootstrap admin credential is the only in-process mint (and :114 lists session tokens on the RFC 8693 path). These are false after SPEC-1/SPEC-6 land, independent of SPEC-7 Edit 5; the non-spec docs loop owns them (DOCS-2 does not cover them).

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Step (1a) re-reads only the issued_tokens rows that exist. The key-set/IdP classification clause is replaced by "a token with no `issued_tokens` row passes this step". CODE-9 item 1 lists the subject and actor jti without classifying them, and fails only on `revoked_at`. — BECAUSE the fail-closed rule rested on the premise that every Lenny-issued token has a row. That premise is false for playground bearers (revoked in Redis `pg:revoked`, spec/27_web-playground.md "Per-request revocation check") and for impersonation bearers (ticket store, pkg/gateway/credentials/impersonation/service.go End). An issued_tokens re-read cannot see their revocation either way, and it rejected every §8.2 delegation whose subject_token is such a bearer (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md "Child-token minting"). The actor always has a row: it is the SPEC-1 session_mint token or a Token Service child. — ALTERNATIVES: (a) apply the missing-row rule only to the external endpoint. This narrows a false premise, and the Token Service has one handler for internal and external calls. (b) Exempt `origin: "playground"` and impersonation bearers. This enumerates exceptions and is hair. (c) Record playground and impersonation bearers in issued_tokens. This is a new mechanism, it needs a dual-write of playground revocation, and a token.exchanged row for a cross-tenant impersonation mint breaks spec/13 "always carries a single tenant_id".
WATCHOUT: Do not restore any missing-row rejection, whether by origin, by caller, or by verifier. The round-1 reason for it ("a revoked playground bearer presented to /v1/oauth/token would mint a fresh recorded token") is a revocation-store gap that predates 0097. The Token Service's `verifyExchangeTokens` (pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go) checks no revocation for subject_token, and §27.3.1 owns playground revocation. A rotation of a row-less subject commits through RecordWithRotationAudit's ErrNoRows branch, and its exp is capped at the subject's exp. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go RecordWithRotationAudit "Previous token absent or already revoked"
OPEN: (security lens, separate finding, predates 0097) Nothing in spec or code applies the §27.3.1 playground revocation check, or the impersonation ticket end, to a subject_token presented on /v1/oauth/token. §13.3 "no grace period" says every validation consults the revocation cache. Whether the Token Service's subject verification is such a validation is unstated.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G2.1]: "Under the fail-closed step (1a), impersonation and playground bearers cannot be exchanged, which matches 'NOT routed through /v1/oauth/token'" is wrong twice. "NOT routed" describes how those bearers are minted, not whether they can be used as a delegation subject. Step (1a) no longer fails closed.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]: the step (1a) fail-closed DECISION is withdrawn (see above). The WATCHOUT on RecordWithRotationAudit's ErrNoRows branch now reads "reachable only for a subject with no row", not "only for an IdP-issued subject".
CORRECTS [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]: the DEFERRED tier-1 pkg/tokenservice case that pins the Lenny-vs-IdP recheck classification is moot. CODE-9 no longer classifies.
DECISION: The §13.3 "Token issuance and rotation" sentence "The bootstrap `lenny-admin` Secret rotation is the one exception ..." becomes a pointer to §10.2 "Signing topology", as new SPEC-7 Edit 5. It names the admin rotation only so the following parenthetical keeps a referent. — BECAUSE "Signing topology" is the one home for the gateway's in-process mint set (D9), and SPEC-7 already owns §13.3, so checklist S7 ("Lands the §13.3 and §8.2 edits") and the Spec-files-touched list stay true. — ALTERNATIVES: list the mints in §13.3 (a second home, and the same enumerating answer one step wider); put the edit in SPEC-6 (S6 "Lands the §4.3 item 3 edit" would then be stale, and the fixer cannot change the checklist).
OPEN: (docs loop) docs/operator-guide/security.md "Serves the canonical POST /v1/oauth/token ..." bullet and its "Bearer tokens" item, and docs/getting-started/architecture.md "Externally, token issuance ...", still name the admin credential as the only in-process mint and list session tokens on the RFC 8693 path. SPEC-6 and SPEC-1 make that false. DOCS-2 does not cover these pages.

FACT: Postgres runs synchronous replication with automatic failover, so a committed session-creation `issued_tokens` row is not lost on failover and the SPEC-7 (1a) missing-row rejection does not fire for tokens already returned — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:30
FACT: §07 session lifecycle states no ordering between the session row commit and the session-creation token mint; the mint is out of scope (summary.md non-goals), so an orphan-session-on-token-write-failure finding has no spec anchor to fix in this proposal — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md (no capability-token mention); spec/10_gateway-internals.md:223
WATCHOUT: `issued_tokens` rows are retained until exp precedes a trailing hour (GC, erasure, and tenant Phase 4 all honor it), so the new (1a) "verified but row missing → reject" rule cannot fire on an unexpired recorded token through cleanup — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:676

### [spec.3.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The §8.2 delegation exchange sets actor_token to the parent session token and subject_token to the user's JWT. Under SPEC-1 "Signing topology" the parent session token always has an issued_tokens row, so the no-row pass in SPEC-7 Edit 1 step (1a) does not weaken the actor freshness rule. A playground or impersonation bearer as subject passes (1a) and is revoked through the playground Redis key, a separate pre-existing mechanism. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:59, spec/27_web-playground.md:155
FACT: The SPEC-7 Edit 5 find-string matches spec/13_security-model.md:616 verbatim. §4.3 item 3's admin carve-out sentence (spec/04_system-components.md:204) uses no "one exception" wording, so SPEC-6 leaving it unchanged is consistent. EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:616

### [spec.3.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding on the r2->r3 diff (SPEC-7 Edit 1 step (1a) now passes any token with no issued_tokens row; new SPEC-7 Edit 5) — BECAUSE Postgres issued_tokens.revoked_at is the sole authoritative revocation store and the revocation cache is fed only from it, so a row-less token (playground, impersonation) was never revocable through that store; passing it at (1a) removes no existing control — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing fail-closed missing-row, rejected as it was the r2 defect that broke delegation from playground/impersonation sessions.
FACT: revocation cache is a latency optimization over issued_tokens; validation falls through to `SELECT revoked_at FROM issued_tokens WHERE jti = $1` — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:674, :676; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:274
FACT: SPEC-7 Edit 5 target text "The bootstrap `lenny-admin` Secret rotation is the one exception: it is a gateway-direct in-process mint that does not go through `/v1/oauth/token`" exists verbatim — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:616

### [spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 3 points the §25.4 "Startup and Readiness" listener sentence at the §10.2 key-set load ("starts its HTTP listener immediately after it loads the verifier key set") — BECAUSE §10.2 (SPEC-2) already states "a process that cannot load a mapped entry does not start"; a citation avoids a second home — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "one exception" sentence (restates SPEC-2); lazy key-set load (contradicts SPEC-2's single startup load).
DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 2 cites §13.3 "Token rotation and revocation" and "Authoritative durability for revocation" for Lenny-issued tokens on lenny-ops, with the fail-closed refusal scoped to Lenny-issued tokens — BECAUSE §13.3's fail-closed sentence names "a gateway replica" and refuses every token; IdP tokens have no issued_tokens row, so refusing them gains nothing — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's literal revoked_at clause (omits cache, propagation, fail-closed); fail-open on stale cache (contradicts §13.3 "we refuse to serve"); gateway introspection RPC (new surface); refusing Lenny-issued tokens on lenny-ops entirely (§25.1 "Issuing a scoped token" needs them).
DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 4 appends "`lenny-ops` refuses Lenny-issued tokens for the duration ([Section 25.4](#254-the-lenny-ops-service) "Authentication")." to the §25.15 "Postgres down" and "Postgres + Redis both down" Impact cells — BECAUSE those rows are lenny-ops' per-dependency impact inventory and would otherwise overstate what a scoped-token caller can do.
FACT: lenny-ops has no revocation read today; buildAuthConfig wires only an HMAC verifier plus optional iss ClaimChecker; authmw.Options already has Revocations and RevocationFreshness hooks, consulted after Verify, and RevocationFreshness.Stale() is applied to every bearer — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-ops/main.go:171-189; pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:195-208, :487, :495
WATCHOUT: the design for this group asked the fixer to add CODE-6 item 6, tier-1 lenny-ops revocation tests, a TEST-1 rotation assertion, a DOCS-2 bullet, and a summary CODE-6 index suffix. The spec-loop grant forbids authoring non-spec content, and no existing non-spec statement is falsified by the spec edit, so none of these landed. The summary CODE-6 index line was left unchanged so it stays true of CODE-6 as written. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 items 1-5 (no revocation item)
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-6 does not implement SPEC-11 Edit 2's revocation check. What is owed: wire authmw.Options.Revocations and RevocationFreshness in cmd/lenny-ops buildAuthConfig from a revocation.Cache rehydrated from issued_tokens and fed by token.revoked, as cmd/lenny-gateway does; scope both checks to tokens the KeySet verified (unscoped wiring 503s IdP and dev-hook callers during a Postgres outage). Tier-1 cmd/lenny-ops tests (// spec: 25.4, 13.3): revoked key-set token -> TOKEN_REVOKED; stale cache -> 503 token_validation_unavailable for a key-set token, while a dev-trust-hook token is admitted (the discriminating case). TEST-1 item 2 Rotation: the replaced admin credential is refused on lenny-ops after step 2. DOCS-2: docs/operator-guide/agent-operability.md failure table rows "Postgres down" and "Postgres + Redis both down" (lines ~356, ~358) state that lenny-ops refuses Lenny-issued tokens while Postgres is unreachable. Summary CODE-6 index line then gains ", and the revocation check".
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S11 says "Lands the §18.33 and §25.4 authentication edits." SPEC-11 now also lands the §25.4 "Startup and Readiness" edit (Edit 3) and the §25.15 Postgres outage rows (Edit 4). Rewrite S11 as the pointer "Lands SPEC-11."
OPEN: spec/25 §25.15 closing paragraph ("even during storage outages (Postgres and/or Redis down), the core operational loop ... continues to function") stays true for IdP-authenticated callers but not for agents holding §25.1 scoped Lenny-issued tokens once SPEC-11 Edit 2 lands. A later round decides whether it needs a caveat or an Edit 4 extension.
OPEN: lenny-ops' new issued_tokens read has no §25.4 "Storage Routing" row, and §12.6 assigns issued_tokens no StoreRouter method for any reader (design marked separate-finding).

### [spec.4.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: Removed the client-side per-replica KMS sign rate limit (`⌊kms.signQuotaPerSecond ÷ (G + T)⌋`, `--kms-sign-rate-limit`, `--kms-sign-burst`, `BreakerSigner.Limit/Burst`, the `rate_limited` outcome, and CHART-3) from the whole proposal. Provider throttling is the only quota enforcement. — BECAUSE an even per-replica split caps the fixed-size Token Service (2/2/4 replicas, no autoscaler) at 4Q/34 of the quota at Tier 3, below its lease-plus-delegation demand, and the limiter was undefined under `kms.provider: local`. The provider throttle already returns the same retryable 503 fleet-wide and does not trip the breaker. — ALTERNATIVES: rejected a per-component Token Service share value (adds a value, validation, docs, and chart formulas, and still only approximates the provider throttle); rejected non-partitioning ceilings ⌊Q/G⌋ and ⌊Q/T⌋ (the fleet can reach 2Q, so the limiter protects nothing); rejected adding only a "not local" condition (leaves the starvation); rejected an optional limiter (a dual mode). The reason lives only in summary D15.
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce a client-side sign rate limit or a static split of `kms.signQuotaPerSecond` across replicas. The Token Service does not autoscale, so any split sized to the gateway HPA maximum starves it. `kms.signQuotaPerSecond` remains solely as the `KMSSignQuotaApproaching` threshold. — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md §17.8.2 table row "| Token Service replicas | 2 | 2 | 4 |"; §17.8.2 Step 3 "`autoscaling.maxReplicas: 30`"; charts/lenny/presets/values-tier3.yaml `tokenService: replicas: 4`.
FACT: CHART-3 is a retired ID. CHART-4 and CHART-5 keep their numbers, and the range phrase "CHART-1 to CHART-5" in non-spec **Design** and the tests heading still stands with the gap. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md "Tests that land with CHART-1 to CHART-5".
FACT: CODE-3 items were renumbered to 1 to 3 (the Limit/Burst item was removed), and CODE-7 items to 1 to 2 (the flag item was removed). No proposal file cited CODE-3 or CODE-7 by item number before this edit (grep `CODE-(3|7).*item`).
DEFERRED [/home/ec2-user/lenny/.claude/worktrees/draft-0097/proposals/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.implementation-checklist.md]: S26 ("CHART-3. Lands the sign-rate flags.") names a deleted deliverable. Remove S26. No other step lists S26 in Depends on.
DEFERRED [/home/ec2-user/lenny/.claude/worktrees/draft-0097/proposals/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.implementation-checklist.md]: S20 says CODE-7 "Lands the outcome mapping, the sign-rate flags, and the sign metrics". It should say "Lands the outcome mapping and the sign metrics", because CODE-7 no longer adds flags.

### [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: lenny-ops applies the §13.3 revocation check (cache + token.revoked + fail-closed while Postgres is unreachable) to Lenny-issued tokens only, stated once in SPEC-11 Edit 2 by pointer to §13.3 "Token rotation and revocation" and "Authoritative durability for revocation" — BECAUSE §13.3 makes Postgres the sole revocation authority and the 10-year bootstrap admin credential is retired through revoked_at at SPEC-2 step 2; scoping to Lenny-issued tokens keeps IdP callers usable on the outage tool — ALTERNATIVES: refuse Lenny-issued tokens on lenny-ops entirely (rejected: §25.1 Scoped Tokens are minted by POST /v1/oauth/token and presented to lenny-ops); honour the last-known cache during a Postgres outage (rejected: contradicts §13.3 fail-closed); new introspection RPC to the gateway (rejected: new surface, existing authmw hooks cover it).
DECISION: §25.4 Startup and Readiness gets a POINT edit ("starts its HTTP listener immediately after it loads the verifier key set of §10.2 ...") rather than an exception sentence — BECAUSE §10.2 already states that a load failure stops the process.
FACT: lenny-ops already uses authmw.Options (cmd/lenny-ops/main.go buildAuthConfig), which carries Revocations and RevocationFreshness hooks; the gateway wires them from revocation.Cache + revocationprop.Propagator with a 30s Rehydrate loop (cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go, revocation.go). Cache.Stale() is true until the first successful Rehydrate and after defaultMaxStaleness=90s — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go:36,157
WATCHOUT: authmw's RevocationFreshness gate fires for EVERY bearer before the revocation lookup (pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:487). Wired unscoped in lenny-ops it refuses non-Lenny-issued callers during a Postgres outage, contradicting SPEC-11 Edit 2's "applies to them".
WATCHOUT: lenny-ops in production verifies no upstream IdP token in code today (only the HMAC trust key; no JWKS/OIDC verifier in cmd/lenny-ops). With the revocation fail-closed, production lenny-ops serves nobody during a Postgres outage until IdP verification exists. SPEC-11 Edit 2's IdP clause is unimplemented — a separate gap, not this group's.
OPEN: issued_tokens has no StoreRouter routing rule for any reader (gateway included); §25.4 Storage Routing has no row for lenny-ops' new issued_tokens read. A later round assigns the routing and adds the row.
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S11 says "Lands the §18.33 and §25.4 authentication edits"; after this fix SPEC-11 also lands the §25.4 Startup and Readiness edit and the §25.15 Postgres-down and Postgres + Redis both down rows. Rewrite as a pointer: "Lands SPEC-11."

DECISION: delete the client-side per-replica KMS sign rate limit (SPEC-4 sentence, SPEC-8 Edit 2 last formula sentence, `rate_limited` outcome, CODE-3 item 2, CODE-7 item 1 flags, CHART-3, its Helm and tier-7a tests, the HPA edge case); the reason lives once in summary D15 — BECAUSE the provider's own throttle enforces the quota exactly and fleet-wide and already maps to the same retryable 503 without tripping the breaker, while any static split of Q refuses demand the quota could serve (gateway below its HPA max; Token Service fixed at 4 replicas at Tier 3, spec/17_deployment-topology.md §17.8.2 "Token Service replicas" row); deleting it also leaves nothing undefined under `kms.provider: local` — ALTERNATIVES: (a) "When kms.provider is not local" condition on the formula (closes finding 1 only); (b) per-component share value `kms.tokenServiceSign...` with ⌊(Q−S)/G⌋ and ⌊S/T⌋ (new value, validation, docs, Helm test; operator must size S; limiter still only approximates the provider throttle); (c) non-partitioning ⌊Q/G⌋ and ⌊Q/T⌋ (fleet can reach 2Q, so the limiter protects nothing).

Filed this round. The fix belongs in the SPEC-8 sentence.

The refutation still stands on D20 scope grounds; I did not re-file it.
FACT: Per-tenant audit advisory lock (§11.7 item 3) bounds per-tenant audit writes at ≥20/s worst case; the SPEC-1 session_mint audit row adds one row per session to an already-synchronous per-session audit path, so it is incremental rather than a new bottleneck. EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:381-382

### [spec.4.review-reliability.1]
FACT: issued_tokens rows (revoked or not) are retained until exp precedes a trailing hour, so SPEC-7 step (1a) "no row passes" cannot be defeated by expired-row GC deleting a revoked row of a still-presentable token — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:676
FACT: concurrent rotation is serialized by the per-tenant audit advisory lock; the rotation grant writes revoked_at on the predecessor inside the same write-before-issue transaction, so the second rotation's (1a) SELECT (after lock acquisition, READ COMMITTED) sees it — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:662, spec-changes.md SPEC-7 Edit 1
FACT: the playground client already re-exchanges at expiresInSeconds minus a 60 s skew budget, which is the "holder refresh lead" SPEC-8 cites — EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md:82

### [owner-decisions-2026-10-23]
DECISION: Owner decision 4 is option B. `lenny-ops` keeps an in-memory revocation set for Lenny-issued tokens, loaded from `issued_tokens` at startup and kept current while Postgres is reachable by the gateway's §13.3 revocation-cache mechanisms. During a Postgres outage it keeps accepting key-set tokens not in the set. SPEC-11 Edit 2 is the one home and cites §13.3 instead of restating it. SPEC-11 Edit 3 adds that the set gates neither the listener nor readiness, which keeps §25.4 "Startup and Readiness" true. SPEC-11 Edit 4 replaces the "refuses Lenny-issued tokens for the duration" sentence in the §25.15 "Postgres down" and "Postgres + Redis both down" rows with acceptance against the pre-outage set plus the cold-start refusal, matching the existing "populated cache" caveat. SPEC-11 Edit 5 is retired, because the `/v1/admin/me` Degradation sentence holds unqualified. With Redis down and Postgres up, the `issued_tokens` reads keep the set current, which follows the §13.3 "Authoritative durability for revocation" fall-back and the gateway's periodic rehydration; with both down the set is frozen. — BECAUSE the owner chose B on 2026-10-23, and §13.3's fail-closed sentence would disable the §25.15 loop — ALTERNATIVES: option A (refuse all Lenny-issued tokens while Postgres is down) and option C (accept only `lenny-ops`-audience tokens and defer revocation), both rejected by the owner.
DECISION: CODE-6 item 6 implements the revocation set: rehydration with the gateway's query, the gateway's propagator, periodic rehydration, `authmw.Options.Revocations` scoped to key-set tokens, and a startup gate that refuses key-set tokens only until the first successful load. The gateway's `RevocationFreshness` gate is not wired. Tests: tier 1 in CODE-6's testing list, tier 4 (non-revoked accepted and revoked refused with Postgres down, cold start refused) in TEST-1 item 2, and tier 9 revocation enforcement in TEST-1 item 6. The tier 4 and tier 9 tests sit in TEST-1 because they need the TEST-1 item 1 harness, and S29 already depends on S18.
DECISION: CODE-6 item 3 now loads the key set in every `lenny-ops` process, with or without `--production`, because an unset `--kms-provider` resolves to `local` in `providerflags.Resolve`. This reconciles CODE-6 with SPEC-2's every-process load.
DECISION: Owner decision 5. `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` is the one default for playground bearers and for `POST /v1/oauth/token` tokens requested without `expires_in`. SPEC-3 Edit 1 states the default and the bounds once. The §27.2 row and the §17.8.1 row name it as a default lifetime of refreshable tokens. CODE-8 item 3 replaces the hard-coded 1-hour `durationFromExpiresIn` default in `pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go` with `Options.CapabilityTokenTTL`, set from a new `lenny-token-service` `--capability-token-ttl-seconds` flag that the chart renders from the same value. CODE-8 item 4 leaves `RequestedExp` zero for an `actor_token` exchange with no `expires_in`, which `tokenexchange.Derive` already treats as absent, so delegated children never take the default. SPEC-8 Edit 2's formula now counts every refreshable-token holder with its own refresh lead. — BECAUSE owner decision 5 (2026-10-23) — ALTERNATIVES: a separate Token Service default value (two values for one lifetime, rejected with the same reasoning as keeping `playground.bearerTtlSeconds`); keeping the 1-hour code default for delegation children (a non-spec constant with no override).
DECISION: The gateway-to-pod renewal path for the session-creation `session_capability` token is recorded as a follow-up proposal in the summary's "Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage" and in the non-goals. D21 keeps its substance, because nothing in this proposal's own contract is deferred.
WATCHOUT: Scope departure, per-replica sign rate limit. The problem statement's scope line "There is also a per-replica sign rate limit" is not implemented (summary D15). Do not re-file it. — EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md "Scope", throttling bullet; summary.md D15
WATCHOUT: Scope departure, §12.2 rows. The problem statement asks for capacity guidance in §17.8 and §12.2 and for token-issuance rows in the Postgres write budget; capacity text lands in §17.8 only (summary D20). — EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md "Scope", capacity bullet; summary.md D20
WATCHOUT: Scope departure, key-identity surface. The problem statement asks for key-identity flags and Helm values; the reserved aliases in the existing `kms.aliases` map replace them (summary D5). — EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md "Scope", key-identity bullet; summary.md D5
WATCHOUT: Scope departure, verifier refresh rule. The problem statement asks for a refresh rule for the verifier key set; the startup-loaded static set replaces it, because pinned key versions are immutable (summary D8). — EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md "Scope", verifier key set bullet; summary.md D8
WATCHOUT: The checklist keeps S27 to S32 at their numbers after S26 was removed, so ledger entries that cite S27 still point at CHART-4.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G1.1]: the DECISION that `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` governs only playground bearers, the WATCHOUT "Do not re-add the /v1/oauth/token renewal clause", and the WATCHOUT that the knob governs exactly the tokens `playground.bearerTtlSeconds` governed are superseded by owner decision 5. The default also governs `POST /v1/oauth/token` tokens requested without `expires_in`. The session-creation token keeps its cap.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]: the DECISION that `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` governs only playground bearers, and the WATCHOUT that the rename is cosmetic, are superseded by owner decision 5 in the same way.
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-G1.1]: the DECISION that SPEC-11 Edit 2 carries the §13.3 fail-closed refusal for Lenny-issued tokens, the DECISION that SPEC-11 Edit 4 appends "`lenny-ops` refuses Lenny-issued tokens for the duration", and the DEFERRED item to wire `RevocationFreshness` in `lenny-ops` are superseded by owner decision 4. The OPEN on the §25.15 closing paragraph is closed (Standing context, Settled).
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]: the DECISION that `lenny-ops` applies the §13.3 check with the fail-closed refusal while Postgres is unreachable is superseded by owner decision 4.
CORRECTS [spec.4.follow-up-fix.1]: SPEC-11 Edit 5 and the summary index naming the `/v1/admin/me` degradation sentence are withdrawn under owner decision 4.
FACT: S7 depends on S1, because SPEC-7 Edit 5 cites the §10.2 "Signing topology" label that SPEC-1 creates (the S7 WATCHOUT in [spec.4.review-applicability.1]). The checklist row is corrected.

### [redesign.1]
DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 2 now holds the `lenny-ops` revocation set as numbered **Revocation set** rules 1 to 6 (startup and 30-second reads that only add, `token.revoked` events, 401, the 503 gate before the first successful read, acceptance after it, and IdP exclusion). SPEC-11 Edits 3 and 4, the spec-changes edge case, D22, the owner-decision-4 Settled bullet, CODE-6 item 6, the CODE-6 tier-1 tests (one case per rule), TEST-1 items 2 and 6, and DOCS-2 cite the rules by number. — BECAUSE the prose form cited the gateway's §13.3 mechanism as a whole, which imports the per-request read and the fail-closed refusal `lenny-ops` must not apply, and §13.3 states no periodic read. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:662,674,676; cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:200-218
DECISION: The missed-revocation window is bounded by the outage rather than by a 1-hour cap, because `lenny-ops` accepts every audience (D22). — EVIDENCE: pkg/ctlcli/ops.go:52-54; cmd/lenny-ops/main.go:171-198; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:433

DECISION: The GatewayClient Open item is settled (projected ServiceAccount token, no `POST /v1/oauth/token` request), and the SPEC-8 GatewayClient refresh-lead example is withdrawn. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/ops-deployment.yaml:168,488-495
DECISION: Four pre-existing defects are recorded under the summary's Defects: the unwired `tokenservice.Options.RevocationPropagator`, the `TOKEN_REVOKED` code drift, the production strict readiness probe, and default-empty `aud` enforcement. Open decisions OD1 (revoked-token code) and OD2 (production readiness during a Postgres outage) are recorded in the summary with default (a) each.
DECISION: Checklist S18 gains tier 2 for the CODE-6 component test, and S31 depends on S11 because DOCS-2 points at SPEC-11 Edit 2 rules. The CODE-6 deliverable index entry names `ops-deployment.yaml`, and a watch-out records the chart DSN dependency.
DECISION: The problem statement's owner decision 4 keeps its 1-hour bound and its "mechanism the gateway's §13.3 revocation cache uses" wording, because it records the owner's input as given; D22 records the departure.
WATCHOUT: Replaced mechanism, "keeps it current by the mechanisms the gateway's revocation cache uses". It lost because §13.3 also carries the per-request `SELECT revoked_at` fallback and the fail-closed refusal, and it states no periodic read. Do not re-cite §13.3's mechanism as a whole.
WATCHOUT: Replaced bound, "a token whose audience is `lenny-ops` lives at most the 1-hour `lenny-ops` per-dialect cap". It lost because `lenny-ctl` presents its gateway bearer to `lenny-ops`, and no verifier enforces `aud` by default.
WATCHOUT: Replaced gate, `Cache.Stale` as the startup gate. It lost because it turns true again after 90 s of failed reads, which is option A.
WATCHOUT: Replaced dev path, a `lenny-ops` without a DSN that wired neither the set nor the gate. It lost because §25.4 rule 4 carries no development exemption.
OPEN: The related-passages lookup returned §13.3, §11.4, §27.3.1, §17.7, the §25.4 GatewayClient lifecycle, and the postgres-failover runbooks as leads. None is made wrong by this redesign, so no spec or docs edit site is added.


### [spec.5.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 2 gains rule 7: `lenny-ops` refuses a Lenny-issued token carrying `origin: "playground"` with `401 UNAUTHORIZED` — BECAUSE playground bearers have no `issued_tokens` row and are revoked only by the Redis `t:{tenant}:pg:revoked:{jti}` marker (spec/27 §27.3.1 "Revocation write", "Per-request revocation check"), so the rule-1/rule-2 set never sees their revocation; no flow sends one to `lenny-ops` (§27.7 CSP `connect-src 'self' wss://<gateway-host>`) — ALTERNATIVES: wiring authmw `PlaygroundRevocations` on `lenny-ops` (adds a Redis hot-path dependency and a fail-closed 503 for tokens no client sends there); a clause on rule 3 or 6 importing §27.3.1 (same cost, plus a conditional on an existing rule).
DECISION: rule 7 is appended (not inserted) so rules 4 and 5 keep their numbers; SPEC-11 Edit 4, the Edge cases line, DOCS-2, and the existing test cases keep their citations.
DECISION: rule 5's reason clause "because no revocation can commit while Postgres is down" deleted — BECAUSE D22 accepts a read fault while Postgres still accepts writes, so the clause was false; D22 is the reason's home. D22's own sentence stays (it holds for every token `lenny-ops` accepts once rule 7 refuses playground bearers).
DECISION: new SPEC-11 Edit 5 adds a KMS row to the §25.4 "Dependencies" table, a pointer sentence to rules 4 and 5 on the Postgres row's Used-by cell, and a KMS bullet to the §25.1 "Component Model" dependency list — BECAUSE SPEC-2 makes KMS a startup dependency of `lenny-ops` and both dependency summaries omitted it. This Edit 5 reuses the number of the retired `/v1/admin/me` Edit 5; the two are unrelated.
FACT: the gateway enforces the playground check in `pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go` (`m.opts.PlaygroundRevocations != nil && claims.Origin == playgroundOriginClaim`); `cmd/lenny-ops/main.go` builds `authmw.Options` with no `PlaygroundRevocations` — EVIDENCE: auth.go:525, main.go:188.
FACT: no docs page mirrors the §25.4 Dependencies table (docs/operator-guide/agent-operability.md has only a failure-modes table) — EVIDENCE: grep -i depend docs/operator-guide/agent-operability.md.
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 item 2's tier-4 acceptance bullet now excludes playground bearers; a later round that adds a cross-process acceptance test must not use a playground bearer against `lenny-ops` — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes TEST-1 item 2.

### [spec.5.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-4 Edit 1 (§10.2 "KMS signing failure mode") is the single home for the KMS_SIGNING_UNAVAILABLE causes, the Retry-After values ("remaining breaker cooldown when open, and 1 second otherwise"), and the outage impact; the §15 row (SPEC-4 Edit 2) is now a pointer with no cause list and no existing-sessions sentence — BECAUSE the §15 cause list was a copy that drifted from §10.2 and omitted the closed-breaker hard error — ALTERNATIVES: adding the hard-error cause to the §15 row (two copies again); narrowing §10.2 so a closed-breaker hard error stays 500 (a KMS 5xx is transient, and §10.2's lead sentence already makes every failure a 503); a typed BreakerSigner error carrying RetryAfter for hard errors (new mechanism; the HTTP-layer floor of 1 already covers it).
FACT: today a hard inner Sign error with the breaker closed reaches 500 server_error on the Token Service, because BreakerSigner.Sign returns the raw inner error and the mint path only maps errors.Is(err, jwt.ErrSigningUnavailable) to 503 — EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go:108-115; pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:705-711. CODE-7 item 1 now maps every Sign error to 503 and the Tier 2 bullet asserts the closed-breaker case with Retry-After: 1.
DECISION: "Existing sessions are unaffected" is withdrawn from SPEC-4 Edit 1, the §15 row, the SPEC-5 Edit 3 alert row, and summary D8; §10.2 now says previously issued tokens keep verifying until exp and a running session's own mints (example: delegation child token) fail until signing recovers — BECAUSE delegation and refresh mints for running sessions call Sign — ALTERNATIVES: naming credential-lease renewal as the example (false: lease tokens are randomString handles, pkg/credential/lease_mint.go:121,142); listing every affected mint (restates other sections).
WATCHOUT: Do not name credential-lease issuance or renewal as a KMS mint anywhere; leases sign nothing. The spec's Token Service outage text (§4.3 "continue operating", §17.7 Token Service runbook, TokenServiceUnavailable alert) concerns Token Service availability and stays true — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:224; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:827; spec/16_observability.md:429.
FACT: problem-statement lines 47 and 104 describe what current §10.2/§15 say ("existing sessions are unaffected") as evidence the spec assumes fixed-lifetime tokens; they remain accurate descriptions of the current spec and were left unedited — EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1135; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:244.


### [spec.5.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: SPEC-8 Edit 2 "KMS Sign rate" premise now reads "Every Lenny-issued JWT costs one KMS `Sign` call"; formula, 230/s and 690/s figures, and the quota sentence are unchanged — BECAUSE credential-lease tokens are opaque TokenStore handles and cost no Sign, so the finding's lease terms would count Sign calls that never happen — ALTERNATIVES: adding lease issue and renewal terms (oversizes the quota); "figures exclude leases" (implies leases sign); a defence sentence in SPEC-8 (restates §13.3 and §4.9).
FACT: Lease tokens sign nothing. §13.3 RFC 8693 table types lease tokens as `access_token`; §4.9 "LLM Reverse Proxy" step 3 validates the lease token against `TokenStore`; `credential.MintLease` builds the proxy-mode token with `randomString("lt", 32)`; direct mode carries no Lenny token. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:625; spec/04_system-components.md:1594; pkg/credential/lease_mint.go:140-145, 187-195
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]: the UNVERIFIED "formula has no credential-lease term" is answered; no lease term is owed. Standing context Open item 1 lost its lease clause; the API-client-holder recheck remains open.
CORRECTS [spec.4.review-performance.1]: same answer for its lease undercount concern.
WATCHOUT: Do not re-file "Sign-rate formula omits credential-lease mints". Leases are opaque handles; only JWT mints reach the KMS. — EVIDENCE: pkg/credential/lease_mint.go:140-145
OPEN: The spec/04 "LLM Reverse Proxy" threat-model clause saying KMS-key control lets an attacker "forge lease tokens entirely" contradicts opaque leases; predates 0097, separate finding.


### [spec.5.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: The Embedded Mode local KMS master key is a Secret that `lenny up` creates in the embedded cluster when it is absent and keeps on AlreadyExists. The chart mounts the Secret that the new `kms.masterKeySecret` value names, the dev preset sets that value to a fixed name, and the lookup-generated Secret renders only when the value is empty (helm install, Kind tier 5). SPEC-9 Edit 1 KMS row, CHART-2 Targets and item 1, the Helm unittest bullet, a new tier-2 envtest bullet, Files touched, and summary D12 and the CHART-2 index line were edited to match. BECAUSE embedded manifests are pre-rendered by `helm template --no-hooks`, `lookup` returns empty there, so a chart-generated key would be fixed per build and shared by every install, and it would also break the tier-11 byte-for-byte sync. This mirrors `security.oidc.bearerTrustKeySecret` and `createDevBearerSecret`. ALTERNATIVES: a pinned dev key in presets/dev.yaml (rejected: it is a live signing key); a host file at `Paths.KMSMasterKey()` (rejected: adds surviving state outside the substrate); requiring `kms.masterKeySecret` (rejected: `kms.provider` defaults to `local`); overwriting a chart-rendered Secret after apply (rejected: still nondeterministic, and a re-apply clobbers the key).
FACT: Embedded manifests come from `helm template --no-hooks` over the dev preset, and TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender fails on any byte difference. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.go:9-25; tests/tier11_docs/embedded_manifests_sync_test.go:56-77
FACT: `createDevBearerSecretFromConfig` does Create and then Update on AlreadyExists. The master key Secret must NOT copy that, because an update would rotate the live signing and KEK key. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/stack/bringup.go:140-166; the call site is stack.go:316
WATCHOUT: Do not re-file "the §17.4 State and resets sentence omits the KMS key". The Secret is held in the embedded cluster's datastore, which is part of the persisted substrate, and `--purge` clears it. Neither that sentence nor SPEC-9 Edit 3 needs an edit. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/stack/lifecycle.go:75-135 (per design; not re-read)

### [spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: lenny-ops refuses every Lenny-issued token carrying `origin: "playground"` with 401 UNAUTHORIZED, appended as SPEC-11 Edit 2 **Revocation set** rule 7 (no renumbering, so Edit 4, Edge cases, DOCS-2 and the tests keep citing rules 4 and 5) — BECAUSE no flow carries a playground bearer to lenny-ops (playground CSP connect-src is the gateway only; §15 playground endpoints are gateway-only; exhaustive meaning search over spec/25, 27, 15 found none), so refusing is smaller than enforcing §27.3.1 — ALTERNATIVES: wiring authmw `PlaygroundRevocations` (Redis GET on `t:{tenant}:pg:revoked:{jti}`, fail-closed 503) on lenny-ops, rejected because it adds a Redis hot-path dependency and a fail-closed 503 on a surface no playground client reaches; folding an exclusion into the Authentication sentence, rejected because "validates ... other than" does not say the outcome.
WATCHOUT: an implementor can realise rule 7 with zero new authmw surface by wiring `authmw.Options.PlaygroundRevocations` on lenny-ops with a checker that always reports revoked; authmw then writes 401 UNAUTHORIZED reason bearer_revoked. That runs after tenant extraction, so before the first read a playground token may get the rule-4 503 instead; the tier-1 rule-7 case asserts after a read for that reason. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:525-538
DECISION: rule 5's "because no revocation can commit while Postgres is down" is deleted rather than replaced — BECAUSE the rule's own clause ("a failed read leaves the set unchanged and a token not in the set is accepted") already states the outcome, and D22 is the reason home and already records the read-fault residual — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's replacement clause, rejected as a second statement of D22's residual risk.

WATCHOUT: the new SPEC-11 Edit 5 reuses the number of the retired `/v1/admin/me` Edit 5 named in Standing context; they are unrelated.
